   function hasConnection() {
                return navigator.connection || navigator.mozConnection || navigator.webkitConnection || navigator.msConnection;
            }

            function createLink(src) {
                var preloadLink = document.createElement("link");
                preloadLink.rel = "preload";
                preloadLink.href = src;
                preloadLink.as = "font";
                preloadLink.type = "font/woff2";
                preloadLink.crossOrigin = "";
                document.head.insertBefore(preloadLink, document.head.firstChild);
            }

            var connectionDetail = {
                saveDataEnabled: false,
                slowConnection: false
            };

            var connection = hasConnection();
            if (connection) {
                connectionDetail.saveDataEnabled = connection.saveData;
                if (/\slow-2g|2g/.test(connection.effectiveType)) {
                    connectionDetail.slowConnection = true;
                }
            }

            if (!(connectionDetail.saveDataEnabled || connectionDetail.slowConnection)) {
                createLink("/static/fonts/HardingText-Regular-Web-cecd90984f.woff2");
            } else {
                document.documentElement.classList.add('save-data');
            }

            var evt = document.createEvent( 'CustomEvent' );

   {"mainEntity":{"headline":"A droplet-based electricity generator with high instantaneous power density","description":"Extensive efforts have been made to harvest energy from water in the form of raindrops1–6, river and ocean waves7,8, tides9 and others10–17. However, achieving a high density of electrical power generation is challenging. Traditional hydraulic power generation mainly uses electromagnetic generators that are heavy, bulky, and become inefficient with low water supply. An alternative, the water-droplet/solid-based triboelectric nanogenerator, has so far generated peak power densities of less than one watt per square metre, owing to the limitations imposed by interfacial effects—as seen in characterizations of the charge generation and transfer that occur at solid–liquid1–4 or liquid–liquid5,18 interfaces. Here we develop a device to harvest energy from impinging water droplets by using an architecture that comprises a polytetrafluoroethylene film on an indium tin oxide substrate plus an aluminium electrode. We show that spreading of an impinged water droplet on the device bridges the originally disconnected components into a closed-loop electrical system, transforming the conventional interfacial effect into a bulk effect, and so enhancing the instantaneous power density by several orders of magnitude over equivalent devices that are limited by interfacial effects. A device involving a polytetrafluoroethylene film, an indium tin oxide substrate and an aluminium electrode allows improved electricity generation from water droplets, which bridge the previously   <meta content="Extensive efforts have been made to harvest energy from water in the form of raindrops1–6, river and ocean waves7,8, tides9 and others10–17. However, achieving a high density of electrical power generation is challenging. Traditional hydraulic power generation mainly uses electromagnetic generators that are heavy, bulky, and become inefficient with low water supply. An alternative, the water-droplet/solid-based triboelectric nanogenerator, has so far generated peak power densities of less than one watt per square metre, owing to the limitations imposed by interfacial effects—as seen in characterizations of the charge generation and transfer that occur at solid–liquid1–4 or liquid–liquid5,18 interfaces. Here we develop a device to harvest energy from impinging water droplets by using an architecture that comprises a polytetrafluoroethylene film on an indium tin oxide substrate plus an aluminium electrode. We show that spreading of an impinged water droplet on the device bridges the originally disconnected components into a closed-loop electrical system, transforming the conventional interfacial effect into a bulk effect, and so enhancing the instantaneous power density by several orders of magnitude over equivalent devices that are limited by interfacial effects. A device involving a polytetrafluoroethylene film, an indium tin oxide substrate and an aluminium electrode allows improved electricity generation from water droplets, which bridge the previously disconnected circuit components." name="dc.description"/>

  <meta content="Extensive efforts have been made to harvest energy from water in the form of raindrops1–6, river and ocean waves7,8, tides9 and others10–17. However, achieving a high density of electrical power generation is challenging. Traditional hydraulic power generation mainly uses electromagnetic generators that are heavy, bulky, and become inefficient with low water supply. An alternative, the water-droplet/solid-based triboelectric nanogenerator, has so far generated peak power densities of less than one watt per square metre, owing to the limitations imposed by interfacial effects—as seen in characterizations of the charge generation and transfer that occur at solid–liquid1–4 or liquid–liquid5,18 interfaces. Here we develop a device to harvest energy from impinging water droplets by using an architecture that comprises a polytetrafluoroethylene film on an indium tin oxide substrate plus an aluminium electrode. We show that spreading of an impinged water droplet on the device bridges the originally disconnected components into a closed-loop electrical system, transforming the conventional interfacial effect into a bulk effect, and so enhancing the instantaneous power density by several orders of magnitude over equivalent devices that are limited by interfacial effects. A device involving a polytetrafluoroethylene film, an indium tin oxide substrate and an aluminium electrode allows improved electricity generation from water droplets, which bridge the previously disconnected circuit components." name="description"/>

    <div aria-hidden="true" class="c-context-bar u-hide" data-context-bar="" data-test="context-bar">

     <div class="c-article-body">
      <section aria-labelledby="Abs1" data-title="Abstract" lang="en">
       <div class="c-article-section" id="Abs1-section">
        <h2 class="c-article-section__title js-section-title js-c-reading-companion-sections-item" id="Abs1">
         Abstract
        </h2>
        <div class="c-article-section__content" id="Abs1-content">
         <p>
          Extensive efforts have been made to harvest energy from water in the form of raindrops
          <sup>
           <a data-test="citation-ref" data-track="click" data-track-action="reference anchor" data-track-label="link" href="#ref-CR1" id="ref-link-section-d111183778e580" title="Lin, Z.-H., Cheng, G., Lee, S., Pradel, K. C. &amp; Wang, Z. L. Harvesting water drop energy by a sequential contact-electrification and electrostatic-induction process. Adv. Mater. 26, 4690–4696 (2014).">
            1
           </a>
           ,
           <a data-test="citation-ref" data-track="click" data-track-action="reference anchor" data-track-label="link" href="#ref-CR2" id="ref-link-section-d111183778e580_1" title="Jeon, S.-B., Kim, D., Yoon, G.-W., Yoon, J.-B. &amp; Choi, Y.-K. Self-cleaning hybrid energy harvester to generate power from raindrop and sunlight. Nano Energy 12, 636–645 (2015).">
            2
           </a>
           ,
           <a data-test="citation-ref" data-track="click" data-track-action="reference anchor" data-track-label="link" href="#ref-CR3" id="ref-link-section-d111183778e580_2" title="Xiong, J. et al. Wearable all-fabric-based triboelectric generator for water energy harvesting. Adv. Energy Mater. 7, 1701243 (2017).">
            3
           </a>
           ,
           <a data-test="citation-ref" data-track="click" data-track-action="reference anchor" data-track-label="link" href="#ref-CR4" id="ref-link-section-d111183778e580_3" title="Jin, S. et al. Large-area direct laser-shock imprinting of a 3D biomimic hierarchical metal surface for triboelectric nanogenerators. Adv. Mater. 30, 1705840 (2018).">
            4
           </a>
           ,
           <a data-test="citation-ref" data-track="click" data-track-action="reference anchor" data-track-label="link" href="#ref-CR5" id="ref-link-section-d111183778e580_4" title="Xu, W. H. et al. SLIPS-TENG: robust triboelectric nanogenerator with optical and charge transparency using a slippery interface. Natl Sci. Rev. 6, 540–550 (2019).">
            5
           </a>
           ,
           <a aria-label="Reference 6" data-test="citation-ref" data-track="click" data-track-action="reference anchor" data-track-label="link" href="/articles/s41586-020-1985-6#ref-CR6" id="ref-link-section-d111183778e583" title="Yu, J., Ma, E. &amp; Ma, T. Exponential energy harvesting through repetitive reconfigurations of a system of capacitors. Commun. Phys. 1, 9 (2018).">
            6
           </a>
          </sup>
          , river and ocean waves
          <sup>
           <a aria-label="Reference 7" data-test="citation-ref" data-track="click" data-track-action="reference anchor" data-track-label="link" href="/articles/s41586-020-1985-6#ref-CR7" id="ref-link-section-d111183778e587" title="Wang, Z. L. New wave power. Nature 542, 159–160 (2017).">
            7
           </a>
           ,
           <a aria-label="Reference 8" data-test="citation-ref" data-track="click" data-track-action="reference anchor" data-track-label="link" href="/articles/s41586-020-1985-6#ref-CR8" id="ref-link-section-d111183778e590" title="Zhu, G. et al. Harvesting water wave energy by asymmetric screening of electrostatic charges on a nanostructured hydrophobic thin-film Surface. ACS Nano 8, 6031–6037 (2014).">
            8
           </a>
          </sup>
          , tides
          <sup>
           <a aria-label="Reference 9" data-test="citation-ref" data-track="click" data-track-action="reference anchor" data-track-label="link" href="/articles/s41586-020-1985-6#ref-CR9" id="ref-link-section-d111183778e594" title="Scruggs, J. &amp; Jacob, P. Harvesting ocean wave energy. Science 323, 1176–1178 (2009).">
            9
           </a>
          </sup>
          and others
          <sup>
           <a data-test="citation-ref" data-track="click" data-track-action="reference anchor" data-track-label="link" href="#ref-CR10" id="ref-link-section-d111183778e598" title="Dhiman, P. et al. Harvesting energy from water flow over graphene. Nano Lett. 11, 3123–3127 (2011).">
            10
           </a>
           ,
           <a data-test="citation-ref" data-track="click" data-track-action="reference anchor" data-track-label="link" href="#ref-CR11" id="ref-link-section-d111183778e598_1" title="Logan, B. E. &amp; Elimelech, M. Membrane-based processes for sustainable power generation using water. Nature 488, 313–319 (2012).">
            11
           </a>
           ,
           <a data-test="citation-ref" data-track="click" data-track-action="reference anchor" data-track-label="link" href="#ref-CR12" id="ref-link-section-d111183778e598_2" title="Siria, A. et al. Giant osmotic energy conversion measured in a single transmembrane boron nitride nanotube. Nature 494, 455–458 (2013).">
            12
           </a>
           ,
           <a data-test="citation-ref" data-track="click" data-track-action="reference anchor" data-track-label="link" href="#ref-CR13" id="ref-link-section-d111183778e598_3" title="Feng, J. et al. Single-layer MoS2 nanopores as nanopower generators. Nature 536, 197–200 (2016).">
            13
           </a>
           ,
           <a data-test="citation-ref" data-track="click" data-track-action="reference anchor" data-track-label="link" href="#ref-CR14" id="ref-link-section-d111183778e598_4" title="Schroeder, T. B. H. et al. An electric-eel-inspired soft power source from stacked hydrogels. Nature 552, 214–218 (2017).">
            14
           </a>
           ,
           <a data-test="citation-ref" data-track="click" data-track-action="reference anchor" data-track-label="link" href="#ref-CR15" id="ref-link-section-d111183778e598_5" title="Xue, G. et al. Water-evaporation-induced electricity with nanostructured carbon materials. Nat. Nanotechnol. 12, 317–321 (2017).">
            15
           </a>
           ,
           <a data-test="citation-ref" data-track="click" data-track-action="reference anchor" data-track-label="link" href="#ref-CR16" id="ref-link-section-d111183778e598_6" title="Huang, Y. et al. Interface-mediated hygroelectric generator with an output voltage approaching 1.5 volts. Nat. Commun. 9, 4166 (2018).">
            16
           </a>
           ,
           <a aria-label="Reference 17" data-test="citation-ref" data-track="click" data-track-action="reference anchor" data-track-label="link" href="/articles/s41586-020-1985-6#ref-CR17" id="ref-link-section-d111183778e601" title="Chen, X. et al. Scaling up nanoscale water-driven energy conversion into evaporation-driven engines and generators. Nat. Commun. 6, 7346 (2015).">
            17
           </a>
          </sup>
          . However, achieving a high density of electrical power generation is challenging. Traditional hydraulic power generation mainly uses electromagnetic generators that are heavy, bulky, and become inefficient with low water supply. An alternative, the water-droplet/solid-based triboelectric nanogenerator, has so far generated peak power densities of less than one watt per square metre, owing to the limitations imposed by interfacial effects—as seen in characterizations of the charge generation and transfer that occur at solid–liquid
          <sup>
           <a data-test="citation-ref" data-track="click" data-track-action="reference anchor" data-track-label="link" href="#ref-CR1" id="ref-link-section-d111183778e605" title="Lin, Z.-H., Cheng, G., Lee, S., Pradel, K. C. &amp; Wang, Z. L. Harvesting water drop energy by a sequential contact-electrification and electrostatic-induction process. Adv. Mater. 26, 4690–4696 (2014).">
            1
           </a>
           ,
           <a data-test="citation-ref" data-track="click" data-track-action="reference anchor" data-track-label="link" href="#ref-CR2" id="ref-link-section-d111183778e605_1" title="Jeon, S.-B., Kim, D., Yoon, G.-W., Yoon, J.-B. &amp; Choi, Y.-K. Self-cleaning hybrid energy harvester to generate power from raindrop and sunlight. Nano Energy 12, 636–645 (2015).">
            2
           </a>
           ,
           <a data-test="citation-ref" data-track="click" data-track-action="reference anchor" data-track-label="link" href="#ref-CR3" id="ref-link-section-d111183778e605_2" title="Xiong, J. et al. Wearable all-fabric-based triboelectric generator for water energy harvesting. Adv. Energy Mater. 7, 1701243 (2017).">
            3
           </a>
           ,
           <a aria-label="Reference 4" data-test="citation-ref" data-track="click" data-track-action="reference anchor" data-track-label="link" href="/articles/s41586-020-1985-6#ref-CR4" id="ref-link-section-d111183778e608" title="Jin, S. et al. Large-area direct laser-shock imprinting of a 3D biomimic hierarchical metal surface for triboelectric nanogenerators. Adv. Mater. 30, 1705840 (2018).">
            4
           </a>
          </sup>
          or liquid–liquid
          <sup>
           <a aria-label="Reference 5" data-test="citation-ref" data-track="click" data-track-action="reference anchor" data-track-label="link" href="/articles/s41586-020-1985-6#ref-CR5" id="ref-link-section-d111183778e613" title="Xu, W. H. et al. SLIPS-TENG: robust triboelectric nanogenerator with optical and charge transparency using a slippery interface. Natl Sci. Rev. 6, 540–550 (2019).">
            5
           </a>
           ,
           <a aria-label="Reference 18" data-test="citation-ref" data-track="click" data-track-action="reference anchor" data-track-label="link" href="/articles/s41586-020-1985-6#ref-CR18" id="ref-link-section-d111183778e616" title="Nie, J. et al. Power generation from the interaction of a liquid droplet and a liquid membrane. Nat. Commun. 10, 2264 (2019).">
            18
           </a>
          </sup>
          interfaces. Here we develop a device to harvest energy from impinging water droplets by using an architecture that comprises a polytetrafluoroethylene film on an indium tin oxide substrate plus an aluminium electrode. We show that spreading of an impinged water droplet on the device bridges the originally disconnected components into a closed-loop electrical system, transforming the conventional interfacial effect into a bulk effect, and so enhancing the instantaneous power density by several orders of magnitude over equivalent devices that are limited by interfacial effects.
         </p>
        </div>
       </div>
      </section>

             Achieving efficient power generation by designing bioinspired and multi-layered interfacial evaporator
            </a>
           </h3>
           <div class="c-article-meta-recommendations" data-test="recommendation-info">
            <span class="c-article-meta-recommendations__item-type">
             Article
            </span>
            <span class="c-article-meta-recommendations__access-type">

            </span>

      </section>
      <script>
       window.dataLayer = window.dataLayer || [];
                window.dataLayer.push({
                    recommendations: {
                        recommender: 'semantic',
                        model: 'specter',
                        policy_id: 'NA',
